## GRAND PRIX SYDNEY 2013

![](https://i.ormos.uk/fd/cd/fdcd9f3461adb62a03935a2cd5d7043a5bc36b833a3c18bbb9bce4f460ac3d59.jpg)

Few would have picked Melbourne's Allen Zhang to win this power packed Top 8, but armed with his lightning-fast Rakdos deck, he made short work of Martin Jůza in the quarterfinals. Jůza was looking to be the first person to win a Grand Prix in four continents, but he'll have another chance later this year at GP Brisbane.

Zhang then faced New Zealand's Zen Takahashi in the semifinals, besting the feisty 15 year-old with his Shred-Freaks in 3 games, to go through to the finals to face one of Australia's finest mages, Justin Cheung.

Cheung's deck was almost perfectly suited to foil Zhang's aggressive beaters, but having watched Cheung wreck Tomoharu Saito in the semifinals with [Electrickery](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Electrickery) and [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), Zhang played play around them perfectly, quickly and forcefully giving Cheung yet another second place finish for his resume.

Congratulations to Allen Zhang, the 2013 Grand Prix Sydney Champion!

## Quarterfinals Feature Match – Martin Jůza vs. Allen Zhang

*by Pip Foweraker*

 

Juza's Top 8 started inauspiciously with a mulligan to 4, and kept a low-curve hand. Juza played his first land on turn 3, while Zhang took advantage of the free turns to drop a [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) and a [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout). Juza conceded a few turns later, having gleaned some small pieces of information about his opponent's deck.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3d/6b/3d6b4ae96431ecb244d05af25be336689f32f6a73a223479c1376d6078af006e.jpg)*Martin Juza: Not enamoured of mulligans to 4 in the Top 8.*

Allen Zhang 1 – Martin Juza 0

Juza's hand was more acceptable this game, but Zhang sent his back. "I guess it must be my turn", smiled Zhang, shaking his head once more and going to 5. Another mulligan drew a round of laughter from the crowd as Zhang kept his 4.

Both players had plays this game – Zhang with a [Thrill-Kill Assassin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrill-Kill+Assassin) and a [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak), Juza with a [Hellhole Flailer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellhole+Flailer), a [Thrill-Kill Assassin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrill-Kill+Assassin) of his own (plus a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee)!) and a [Mizzium Mortars](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mizzium+Mortars) to clear out Zhang's Assassin.

Zhang started mustering a defense with a [Dead Reveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Reveler), who teamed up with the Shred-Freak to block the Flailer. Unfortunately for Zhang, Juza had more creatures, and Zhang didn't.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/be/c2/bec2aee52186fce57733e70daf2221a3875ac82af54f16b8ef7224dda6f27bfd.jpg)*Zhang: Also not enamoured of mulligans to 4 in the Top 8.*

Martin Juza 1 – Allen Zhang 1

Both players sat back to consider their hands for the decider. Juza thought long and hard about his opening grip before sending it back – 2 off-colour lands not enough for him. Zhang kept his 7, and the players began.

"This is just like last game, but with more cards", remarked Zhang, as he summoned a [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak). Juza had one of his own, and they traded a moment later. Zhang summoned a [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) and a [Viashino Racketeer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Racketeer), while Juza found a [Lobber Crew](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lobber+Crew) to shore up his defenses, then followed it with a [Bloodfray Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodfray+Giant) once he found his 4th land. Zhang had a [Dreadbore](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreadbore) to kill the Giant and attacked, whittling away at Juza's life total. An [Auger Spree](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Auger+Spree) killed the Weird, but Zhang kept up the pressure with a [Tenement Crasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tenement+Crasher).

Juza summoned a [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout), buying himself some time... [Chaos Imps](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chaos+Imps) arrived on Juza's side of the battlefield (unleashed, too!), pushing Zhang to attack and knock out the Roustabout. Zhang had a Roustabout of his own, and Juza dropped a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) onto his [Lobber Crew](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lobber+Crew).

With the [Chaos Imps](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chaos+Imps) threatening a lethal swing the following turn, Zhang pushed all his men sideways. He got through for enough damage that the [Explosive Impact](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explosive+Impact) in his hand was enough to seal the deal.

Allen Zhang 2 – Martin Juza 1

* * *

## Quarterfinals Feature Match – Justin Cheung vs. Adam Witton

*by Pip Foweraker*

 

Cheung led the action with a Seeker of Secrets, while Witton summoned a [Stonefare Crocodile](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonefare+Crocodile). An [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) killed off the pesky reptile and let Cheung connect with his Seeker. Witton rebuilt with an [Ogre Jailbreaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ogre+Jailbreaker).

Cheung passed with no action, and Witton summoned a [Dreg Mangler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreg+Mangler) and attacked with his Jailbreaker. Cheung took the damage and [Annihilating Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annihilating+Fire)d the [Dreg Mangler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreg+Mangler), but Witton had a [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth) in response.

Cheung summoned an [Isperia's Skywatch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Isperia%27s+Skywatch), while Witton had the powerful [Collective Blessing](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Collective+Blessing) to bolster his team. Cheung cast a [Paralyzing Grasp](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Paralyzing+Grasp) on Witton's tapped [Ogre Jailbreaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ogre+Jailbreaker) and summoned a [Cobblebrute](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobblebrute).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e5/4d/e54d9c93be7b283411fc4c2e729ccc12e780e1404ffd0c584f2c95d9c587110d.jpg)*Adam Witton*

Witton had more creatures coming, with a [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and a [Sluiceway Scorpion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sluiceway+Scorpion) bolstering his team. Cheung, on the ropes, watched as Witton attacked with his team the following turn. His [Cobblebrute](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobblebrute) blocked the Scorpion and a [Trostani's Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trostani%27s+Judgment) took care of the [Dreg Mangler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreg+Mangler), dropping Cheung to a perilous 5 life but leaving Witton with only the Shaman available for attacks.

A second [Isperia's Skywatch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Isperia%27s+Skywatch) kept Cheung out of harm's way for a turn, leaving him enough time to summon an Electromancer and [Bloodfray Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodfray+Giant), while Witton had no plays on his turn. Cheung's Skywatch had been crashing through the air unhindered, and within the space of a turn cycle, Witton found himself firmly on the defensive. When the top of his deck had no answers, both players turned to their sideboards.

Justin Cheung 1 – Adam Witton 0

Cheung opened the game with a [Nivix Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nivix+Guildmage) and a [Cobblebrute](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobblebrute), while Witton summoned an [Ogre Jailbreaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ogre+Jailbreaker) and followed it with a [Lotleth Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotleth+Troll). Witton tried a [Dreg Mangler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreg+Mangler), but an [Annihilating Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annihilating+Fire) took care of it quick-smart. Witton attacked with his Jailbreaker, and Cheung declined to block, summoning a [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) on his turn to clear the board temporarily for an attack. Both players nibbled away at each other's life totals, preferring to race rather than trade.

Witton rebuilt with his Troll and an attendant [Wild Beastmaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Beastmaster), then sent in his Jailbreaker. Cheung cast an [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) on the (regenerating) [Lotleth Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotleth+Troll), then copied it with his Guildmage to take out the [Beast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Beast)master. Niiiiice! The Jailbreaker attacked once more and Cheung declined to block, life totals standing at Cheung 5, Witton 4. Cheung summoned a [Batterhorn](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterhorn) and a [Psychic Barrier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychic+Barrier), while Witton stockpiled cards in his hand before summoning a [Sluiceway Scorpion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sluiceway+Scorpion).

Cheung summoned an Isperia's Skyknight, detaining Witton's creatures and swinging with his [Batterhorn](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterhorn) and [Cobblebrute](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobblebrute). Witton cast a [Golgari Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Charm) to 2-for-1 Cheung, killing off both his attackers thanks to the Scorpion's deathtouch. His return attack knocked out Cheung's [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder). Cheung attacked back with his Skyknight, dropping Witton to 1 and leaving his [Psychic Barrier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychic+Barrier) and [Nivix Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nivix+Guildmage) to stare down Witton's Scorpion, Jailbreaker and Troll, all of whom came crashing in.

Cheung blocked Witton's [Lotleth Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotleth+Troll) with his [Psychic Barrier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychic+Barrier), which Witton killed with a [Launch Party](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Launch+Party). Cheung went to ping the Troll with a Staticaster, but Witton had a [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth) to leave it trampling over the now-dead blocker for exactly lethal..

Adam Witton 1 – Justin Cheung 1

Cheung had the first action of the decider, unleashing a [Gore-House Chainwalker](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gore-House+Chainwalker). Witton summoned a [Korozda Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Korozda+Guildmage), while Cheung piled on the pressure with a [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake). The Guildmage attacked unblocked, and post-combat, Witton had an [Axebane Guardian](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Axebane+Guardian).

Cheung attacked with both his creatures and Witton took the damage. Post-combat, Cheung summoned a [Nivix Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nivix+Guildmage). Witton attacked again with his Guildmage and summoned a [Sluiceway Scorpion](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sluiceway+Scorpion). Cheung summoned a [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) and bounced Witton's Scorpion before attacking with his Chainwalker and Drake. Witton threw his [Axebane Guardian](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Axebane+Guardian) in front of the Chainwalker, who promptly [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth)'d himself to live to defend another day.

On his turn, Witton summoned a [Wild Beastmaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Beastmaster) and an unleashed [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout). Cheung had an [Isperia's Skywatch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Isperia%27s+Skywatch), clearing the road once again for his Guildmage and [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake) to attack unhindered. Witton fell to 6 life.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/84/13/84130a07745bbb776a0ff7649e3ba70921c9d110d0a8751e549bd1d4c73fb67d.jpg)*Justin Cheung*

Witton cast a [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth) on his [Wild Beastmaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Beastmaster) and then attacked with it, his [Korozda Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Korozda+Guildmage), and his [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout). Cheung blocked the Guildmage with his [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) and took 10, falling to 6 as well.

Cheung attacked with his Skyknight and passed. At the end of his turn, Witton sacrificed his Roustabout to make 2 [Saproling](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Saproling)s. In response to Witton's all-in attack, Cheung cast an overloaded [Chemister's Trick](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chemister%27s+Trick). Witton could only pump his [Beast](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Beast)master twice, returning a state of balance to the Force – but not quite enough to finish Cheung off.

Justin Cheung 2 – Adam Witton 1

* * *

## Quarterfinals Feature Match – Dan Unwin vs. Tomoharu Saito

*by Ray "blisterguy" Walkinshaw*

 

Dan Unwin had his back against the wall early, with 2 losses to his name at the end of round 6. Needing 8 straight wins, he did just that, coming into the top 8 in first place, for his 3rd straight Australian Grand Prix Top 8.

But if he was looking for an easy way through his Top 8, he wouldn't find one, faced with none other than Japan's Tomoharu Saito, who is certainly no stranger to Grand Prix Top 8's.

Having finished at the top of the Swiss, Unwin was more than happy to play first, but had nothing but lands for his first four turns, while Saito summoned a [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird), and followed it up with a [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c0/4b/c04b885bf8f97fffca94e248601a1584fde82609bf24713c82743689c3f49045.jpg)*Dan Unwin*

Wary of Unwin's untapped lands, Saito finally decided to play an [Ethereal Armor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ethereal+Armor) on his Bird token, before attacking for 7, pumping his Weird twice to drop Unwin to 12.

Unwin finally summoned a [Golgari Longlegs](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Longlegs), but Saito pushed another 7 damage through with an [Inaction Injunction](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inaction+Injunction), before Unwin played a [Knightly Valor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knightly+Valor) on his Longlegs. It was soon sent packing by an [Azorius Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azorius+Charm), and a [Runewing](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runewing) joined the Bird Token, dropping Unwin to just 2 life. An [Eyes in the Skies](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eyes+in+the+Skies) from Saito was enough to convince Unwin to go to game 2.

Saito 1 – Unwin 0

Unwin began game 2 a little faster this time, summoning a turn 3 [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds), but it was already facing down a [Dryad Militant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dryad+Militant) and a New-Prahv Guildmage from Saito. Unwin made a 3/3 with [Slime Molding](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slime+Molding), but Saito kept coming with a [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul), a [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake), and a [Sunspire Griffin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunspire+Griffin), knocking Unwin down to 14.

But this time, Unwin had [Eyes in the Skies](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eyes+in+the+Skies), populating his 3/3 Slime Token and attacking back to keep up with Saito's damage. Saito slowed the bleeding with an [Inaction Injunction](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inaction+Injunction), but Unwin pushed the point with a [Common Bond](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Common+Bond), Saito falling to 11 to Unwin's 10.

Unwin attacked, trading a [Golgari Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Keyrune) for the [Dryad Militant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dryad+Militant), and crushing a [Frostburn Weird](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frostburn+Weird) with his now 4/4 Slime Token, dropping Saito to 9. Saito cast [Knightly Valor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knightly+Valor) on his [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake), and attacked back, putting Unwin to a paltry 4 life.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ef/26/ef266e7630e8b93cbe449988ab855d90ab4d12ef61422a42989c6639622dc74b.jpg)*Tomoharu Saito*

Unwin drew and immediately turned his 4/4 Slime, 3/3 Slime, and 2/3 [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds) sideways. Saito fed his Knight token to the 4/4, and pushed the 4/3 Drake in front of the 3/3, pumping it to try and keep it alive, but Unwin pushed right through it with a [Selesnya Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Selesnya+Charm), dropping Saito to 6.

Saito attacked with his remaining Griffin and Guildmage, and Unwin played the last card in his hand, [Rootborn Defenses](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rootborn+Defenses), to populate his Slime token, but Saito simply gave his Guildmage flying and Unwin extended the handshake with a smile.

Tomoharu Saito defeats Dan Unwin 2-0

"I should have mulliganed game one," Unwin laughed as they packed up their cards. "It was 3 good cards and 4 lands," he shrugged.

* * *

## Quarterfinals Feature Match – Stanislav Cifka vs Zen Takahashi

*by Liam Fraser-Quick*

 

Under the eyes of an enthralled crowd, Pro Tour *Return to Ravnica* champion Stanislav Cifka sat across the table from Zen Takahashi, who only two months ago placed 11th at Grand Prix Auckland. Before the match, Cifka expressed some dissatisfaction with his deck, and was noted to sigh on more than one occasion through his match.

Cifka took a mulligan, but led the action with an [Azorius Arrester](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azorius+Arrester). Takahashi summoned a [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake), while Cifka missed his third land drop, swinging with his [Arrest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arrest)er.

Cifka's [Arrest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arrest)er tried again, only to be ambushed by Takahashi's [Hussar Patrol](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hussar+Patrol). Takahashi continued building his board with [Armory Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armory+Guard), while Cifka (still on two lands) entered emergency mode with a [Slime Molding](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slime+Molding) to make a 1/1.

Takahashi put Cifka to 8 life with one big attack and cemented his already formidable position with an [Izzet Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Keyrune), a [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds) and her pet. A desperate Cifka drew, saw nothing, sighed, and conceded.

Zen Takahashi 1 - Stanislav Cifka 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/43/27/4327f1b11e3360f11caca1a4e66ae3f867f74f2cab038301cb0520f7aa9a2983.jpg)*Takahashi indulges in a moment of Zen.*

Cifka elected to play first, but it was Takahashi who had the first play in [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper), while Cifka kicked off with a [Chromatic Lantern](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chromatic+Lantern), helping fuel a 4/4 Ooze from [Slime Molding](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slime+Molding).

Takahashi laid down an [Armory Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armory+Guard) and Cifka followed with a [Gatecreeper Vine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gatecreeper+Vine). Cifka's [Eyes in the Skies](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eyes+in+the+Skies) made another 4/4 plus a complementary 1/1 flier. Takahashi went onto the back foot with a second [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper), threatening to mill Cifka for 6 cards a turn.

Cifka placed [Armory Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armory+Guard) under [Arrest](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arrest) and got in for 9 with the team for two successive turns, putting Takahashi to 2. He cemented his board position with an [Archweaver](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archweaver). A timely [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) for Takahashi sent an Ooze into the abyss - however, not reading that Cifka's [Archweaver](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archweaver) had trample cost Takahashi the game on the following turn.

Zen Takahashi 1 - Stanislav Cifka 1

A passive opening consisted of Takahashi's [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper) being met by two of Cifka's [Gatecreeper Vine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gatecreeper+Vine)s. The [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper) milled a [Golgari Decoy](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Decoy), threatening to turn any old creature into something much more threatening.

And threaten Cifka did, summoning a [Towering Indrik](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Towering+Indrik). Takahashi decided that a potential 4/6 with reach was a little too much to deal with, and denied Cifka his fun with a [Syncopate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Syncopate). Cifka sighed again. Takahashi's [Izzet Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Keyrune) hit the table, while Cifka played a third [Gatecreeper Vine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gatecreeper+Vine) to add to his already impressive array of shrubberies.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/28/7b/287b2908717c976e8942c8f704a52db2065eda24575a561583656e8d54de469c.jpg)*Cifka demands you fetch him a Shrubbery. One that looks nice. And not too expensive.*

Takahashi broke the stalemate with a [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds) and the obligatory pet Bird token while Cifka (who clearly didn't want a repeat of game one's lack of mana) played a [Chromatic Lantern](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chromatic+Lantern). Takahashi stole first blood with his Bird and followed it up with an even bigger bird in the form of [Archon of the Triumvirate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+the+Triumvirate), which was met with yet another sigh from Cifka as he played his seventh land and passed the turn.

Takahashi put Cifka to 16 with his fliers and played an [Armory Guard](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armory+Guard). Cifka had another [Towering Indrik](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Towering+Indrik), looking very pale in comparison to Takahashi's flying, detaining Archon.

Takahashi swung in with everyone, and Cifka killed off the animated Keyrune with a [Giant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Growth)ed [Gatecreeper Vine](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gatecreeper+Vine). Cifka went to 7. Cifka summoned a [Volatile Rig](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volatile+Rig), but Takahashi's end step [Hussar Patrol](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hussar+Patrol), followed by an overloaded [Blustersquall](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blustersquall), sealed the deal. Cifka – you guessed it – sighed and extended his hand.

Zen Takahashi wins 2-1.

* * *

## Semifinals Feature Match – Allen Zhang vs. Zen Takahashi

*by Pip Foweraker*

 

Zhang led the action with a [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak), while Takahashi summoned a [Concordia Pegasus](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Concordia+Pegasus). Zhang bolstered his Shred-Freak with a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee), while Takahashi summoned a [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds). Zhang killed the Pegasus with a [Dreadbore](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreadbore), swung with his Freak - who traded with both of Takahashi's creatures – and summoned a [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout).

Takahashi had a [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper), but Zhang had another [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) to improve his Roustabout's offense. Takahashi summoned a [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake) but was tapped out, letting Zhang sneak in for another 4. Zhang then summoned a [Viashino Racketeer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viashino+Racketeer), while Takahashi passed without any action on his turn.

Zhang attacked again with his Roustabout, and this time the [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper) jumped in its way. Zhang swung in with both his creatures, and a flashed [Hussar Patrol](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hussar+Patrol) took out the Racketeer. Takahashi attacked with his [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake) and passed. When Zhang's Roustabout attacked again, the Patrol chump-blocked it, but with nothing on the top of Takahashi's library, it was time for sideboards.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/55/e5/55e55b8f5df94d04e4faee2152d34e4ee82d4c8cc6127679a16894152dab949a.jpg)*Allen Zhang*

Allen Zhang 1 – Zen Takahashi 0

Zhang cast the first spell with a [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak), but Takahashi [Syncopate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Syncopate)d it and summoned a [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul). Zhang had a second Shred-Freak, and Takahashi summoned a [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds). Hang enchanted his Shred-Freak with a [Pursuit of Flight](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pursuit+of+Flight), then cast a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) to turn his Shred-Freak into some sort of Hulk.

Takahashi took the 6 damage, then cast an [Avenging Arrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avenging+Arrow) to take out the aptly-named Freak.

On his turn, Takahashi summoned a [Palisade Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Palisade+Giant), which Zhang wiped out with a [Dreadbore](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dreadbore). Takahashi was unfazed, casting a [Knightly Valor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knightly+Valor) on his [Seller of Songbirds](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seller+of+Songbirds) and swinging. With no creatures and an army opposing him, Zhang scooped up his cards and started shuffling.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b8/0d/b80d378a03ffa8c1ba2c462a160824bffe2a971f4888a9134ffc49a12501ea38.jpg)*Zen Takahashi*

Zen Takahashi 1 – Allen Zhang 1

After exchanging comments about Hobbits (both players hail in their youths from NZ), Takahashi took a mulligan and the game began.

Zhang unleashed a [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout), while Takahashi had 2 [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) but no action. Zhang cast a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) on his Roustabout and attacked, while Takahashi missed his 3rd and 4th land drops. Zhang summoned a [Tenement Crasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tenement+Crasher), which Takahashi could tap for a turn with a [Dynacharge](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dynacharge), but Zhang was too far ahead for it to matter.

Allen Zhang 2 – Zen Takahashi 1

* * *

## Semifinals Feature Match – Tomoharu Saito vs. Justin Cheung

*by Ray "blisterguy" Walkinshaw*

 

Justin Cheung is no stranger to Australian **Magic** success, having represented Australia twice in team play, and finishing 2nd at the World Team Championships in 2008. But when it comes to teams, Tomoharu Saito's got him beat, with a Team Pro Tour win in Charleston 2006.

Cheung screamed out of the gates in game one with a [Goblin Electromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Electromancer), a [Stealer of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stealer+of+Secrets), and an unleashed [Bloodfray Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodfray+Giant), adding insult to Saito's mulligan to 4. When Saito finally found his second color, he could only laugh as his New-Prahv Guildmage was promptly shot down by an [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/01/d7/01d7b4857211a5d67a3bb12c55800c1186e4b31ca2de727529cd797b91f9ad88.jpg)*Tomoharu Saito*

"Next game!" Saito announced, his shoulders bouncing with mirth.

Cheung 1 – Saito 0

Cheung attacked early with his [Nivix Guildmage](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nivix+Guildmage), clearing the first [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul) aside with [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm), but applying the brakes in the face of the second, summoning a [Goblin Electromancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Electromancer).

Saito [Inaction Injunction](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inaction+Injunction)'d the Guildmage, and attacked, before summoning a [Dryad Militant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dryad+Militant). Saito chuckled as Cheung added a Staticaster to his team, knocking out the Militant. The [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul) attacked again, and was joined by a [Sunspire Griffin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunspire+Griffin). Cheung attacked with his 2/2's, the Staticaster allowing the Guildmage to trade with the 2/3 Griffin. Cheung followed combat with a [Stealer of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stealer+of+Secrets) and passed it back.

Saito pointed a second [Inaction Injunction](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inaction+Injunction) at the Stealer and attacked with his [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul), the scores now 14 apiece. Cheung attacked back for 2 with his Electromancer, and summoned a [Batterhorn](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterhorn). Saito elected not to attack after playing a 6th land turn, and motioned Cheung to proceed.

[Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) bounced the [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul), and Cheung attacked with his Stealer, [Batterhorn](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterhorn), and Electromancer. A largely unsurprising [Skyline Predator](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skyline+Predator) ambushed the Stealer, and Saito fell to 6 life.

Saito replayed his Soul, but [Isperia's Skywatch](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Isperia%27s+Skywatch) detained the Predator before Cheung attacked with his team. Some [Swift Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swift+Justice) allowed Saito's [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul) to take down the [Batterhorn](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterhorn), the lifelink keeping him afloat. Saito untapped, and summoned a [Tower Drake](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tower+Drake).

With Saito's Drake, Predator, and [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul) facing down Cheung's Staticaster, [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder), Skywatch, and Electromancer, they were at a bit of an impasse. Saito tried to break the silence with a [Detention Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Detention+Sphere) on the Staticaster, but it elected to pass Go and collect $200 anyway, borrowing some [Annihilating Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annihilating+Fire) to help take down the Predator. Saito then tapped out to summon a [Soulsworn Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soulsworn+Spirit), detaining the Skywatch, and letting the Drake fly over the [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) and Electromancer.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/dd/cb/ddcb1141d0a5cea3b741c009faf1066a6416cb47fa341501516752ae0b3890d3.jpg)*Justin Cheung*

With Saito's shields down, Cheung overloaded some [Electrickery](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Electrickery) on the Drake and the [Soulsworn Spirit](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soulsworn+Spirit). Now left with just the [Vassal Soul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vassal+Soul) facing off against Cheung's Skywatch, Saito's low life total was going to be a problem. He bought a turn with a [Dramatic Rescue](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dramatic+Rescue), but Cheung couldn't be stopped.

"Your day!" Saito declared with a grin and a handshake, congratulating the Australian.

**Justin Cheung defeats Tomoharu Saito 2-0 to advance to the finals.**

* * *

## Finals Feature Match – Justin Cheung vs. Allen Zhang

*by Pip Foweraker*

 

### Game 1

Zhang came steaming out of the gates with a [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak), which Cheung killed with an [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm). Cheung summoned a [Psychic Barrier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychic+Barrier), and Zhang unleashed a [Dead Reveler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dead+Reveler). Cheung upped the pace with a [Cobblebrute](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobblebrute), while Zhang had a [Desecration Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Desecration+Demon).

Cheung attacked with his [Cobblebrute](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cobblebrute), which battled the Demon with the aid of some [Annihilating Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Annihilating+Fire). Zhang summoned a [Slum Reaper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slum+Reaper) to clear out Cheung's [Psychic Barrier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychic+Barrier), then bolstered it with a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7f/bc/7fbc0750ab903c975e7a51ee6fb75d79f432dd99597f6f35ab898bfa219ddfe8.jpg)*Justin Cheung*

On his turn, Cheung summoned a [Stealer of Secrets](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stealer+of+Secrets). Zhang attacked with his Gleeful [Reap](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reap)er, then summoned a [Golgari Longlegs](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Longlegs). Cheung had no action on his turn, chump-blocking Zhang's Longlegs when he attacked next turn. Post-combat, Zhang had a [Thrill-Kill Assassin](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrill-Kill+Assassin), which was enough to prompt Cheung to scoop.

Allen Zhang 1 – Justin Cheung 0

### Game 2

Cheung took a mulligan in the second game. Zhang had the first creature with an unleashed [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout), while Cheung missed his 3rd land drop. Zhang cast a [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) on the Roustabout and attacked. Cheung again failed to hit a land, while Zhang cast and equipped a [Civic Saber](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Civic+Saber). At the end of Zhang's turn, Cheung cast an [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) to dig for lands. Hitting at least one, Zhang dropped a [Paralyzing Grasp](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Paralyzing+Grasp) on the Roustabout.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d6/1f/d61f64cb6c2f137d4e761220ebb8d302280463fdad054f3708eae5e3646b91aa.jpg)*Allen Zhang*

Zhang summoned a [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak), equipped it with the Saber, and attacked. Cheung summoned his [Psychic Barrier](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Psychic+Barrier), which Zhang killed with a [Launch Party](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Launch+Party), letting him attack again with his Shred-Freak. Cheung summoned a [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder) to bounce Zhang's Shred-Freak.

On his turn, Zhang re-summoned his [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak) and followed up with a [Slum Reaper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slum+Reaper), swinging for exactly lethal.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9b/d2/9bd282e43daa7d2878902c047bd85b858f305db5d767ff7d0fe048d050d8b740.jpg)*The handshake.*

### Congratulations to Allen 'Jub-Jub' Zhang, your Grand Prix: Sydney Champion!

### Allen Zhang 2 – Justin Cheung 0

* * *

* * *

## Top 5 Cards

 

After a yet another Grand Prix's finals were dominated by aggressive Rakdos cards, the event coverage staff have decided to pay due respect to what is clearly the best guild. Without further ado, here are the Top 5 cards from the winning-est, face-smashing-est decks from the weekend.

 

![Civic Saber](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Civic+Saber)

 

### 5\. [Civic Saber](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Civic+Saber)

[Civic Saber](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Civic+Saber) is a wonderful card for aggressive decks, particularly in Rakdos and Azorius decks. Combining high-powered multicolour creatures with an aggressive and defensive boost helps one's early drops trade up. Grand Prix winner Allen Zhang used his to good effect, giving his creatures the vital points of power to push past [Doorkeeper](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doorkeeper)s and [Voidwielder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voidwielder)s.

  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![Bloodfray Giant](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Bloodfray+Giant)

 

### 4\. [Bloodfray Giant](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodfray+Giant)

The hyper-aggressive Giant is a keystone of any deckbuilder lucky enough to have one. Less effective on defense, but since when does that matter to a Red mage? The Giant's unleash gives you some strategic flexibility, but most of the time, players will opt to summon him in battle stance.

  
  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![Traitorous Instinct](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Traitorous+Instinct)

 

### 3\. [Traitorous Instinct](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Traitorous+Instinct)

Oh, what's that? Your opponent summoned something bigger than your [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler) and [Grim Roustabout](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Roustabout)? That's OK – a [Traitorous Instinct](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Traitorous+Instinct)s will (temporarily) cure what ails you, with the added bonus of extra points of smackdown with your borrowed creature. Your opponents will either learn their lesson about playing big creatures, or you'll beat them to death with them. Either way, it's a very Rakdos way to win.

  
  
  
  
  
  

 

![Deviant Glee](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Deviant+Glee)

 

### 2\. [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee)

Try to stop a smile spreading as you cast this on a creature and swing for 4 before your opponent has laid a blocker. We dare you. It's harder than you think – normally, creature enchantments are mediocre in Limited environments. In RTR Limited, though, enchantments have gained some respect, with many pro's over the weekend voicing admiration for [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee), [Knightly Valor](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knightly+Valor) and a few other enchantments. The hyper-aggressive nature of [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) fits into Rakdos' fairly linear game plans – mostly involving knives and flame – and is a natural fit for Limited decks.

  
  
  

 

![Rakdos Shred-Freak](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak)

 

### 1\. [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak)

Belittled throughout the format by many top players, [Rakdos Shred-Freak](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Shred-Freak) does appear fragile at first glance. He doesn't do much in combat, and there are of creatures out there larger than him. That said, he is fast -very fast – and as Allen Zhang proved, a little faith and a couple of copies of [Deviant Glee](https://web.archive.org/web/20220702204256/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deviant+Glee) showcases the power of The Little Common That Could.
